● Steps 1–5 are done. Here's the checkpoint.

  What this project is

  A port of Circle Defense, a Flash/AS3 wave-defense game (tank in a walled
  arena, waves of circular enemies, money → shop upgrades, 405 levels across
  worlds), to a modern web stack aimed eventually at iOS/Android via Capacitor.
  Stack is Vite 6 + React 19 + TypeScript strict + Phaser 3.90 + Zustand 5,
  tested with Vitest.

  The architectural spine is a hard React↔Phaser separation: one typed event bus
  (GameEventMap in src/game/events/GameEvents.ts), with src/state/bridge.ts as
  the only event→store translator. React never holds a Scene reference. React
  owns the <canvas>; Phaser gets it via config.canvas with parent: null and an
  explicit renderer type, because RESIZE mode would otherwise size the backing
  store in CSS pixels and render at 1/3 res on a phone.

  The SWFimported/ vs NewVersion/ split

  Not peers — a source and a target.

  - SWFimported/ is the JPEXS decompilation of the original SWF: 643 .as files,
  123 MP3s, 1015 SVGs, plus symbolClass/symbols.csv. It's the specification
  being ported from, and it's read-only. The leading number on every extracted
  file is the SWF library ID and is the only link back to symbols.csv, so
  renaming one severs the provenance — there's a test enforcing it.
  - NewVersion/ is the actual project, and all npm commands run from there.
  src/assets/ is gitignored and populated by npm run assets:sync from
  SWFimported/ — already synced here.

  The reason the split matters procedurally: correctness claims in this port are
  settled by citing an AS3 line, not by reasoning about what a name suggests.
  CLAUDE.md's pitfall sections are mostly scar tissue from violating that — a
  field called dead that was the enemy spawn interval, four enemy mechanics
  invented from their names, three weapons declared "blocked" on the basis of
  how exotic their descriptions sounded.

  Baseline

  Clean. npm run typecheck passes both tsconfigs; npm test is 62 files / 1327 
  tests, all passing; npm run progress:check reports PROGRESS.md up to date.
  Working tree was clean at handover — no work in progress from the previous
  session. On develop, pushed and tracking.

  Two contradictions I'm flagging rather than reconciling

  1. PROGRESS.md's hand-written prose is stale (its generated structure is fine
  — progress:check only validates the class list against the AS3 file set, not
  the prose or the statuses):

  - It says eight primaries "each carry their own mechanic and need dedicated
  sessions." All twelve are WeaponSpec constants in
  src/game/weapons/firing.ts:161-577 with dedicated test files.
  - It says "No secondaries are ported." src/game/weapons/secondaries.ts is 214
  lines with 28 passing tests.
  - It says of the enemy behaviour loop "none of it is ported." enemyFiring.ts,
  statusEffects.ts and enemyDeath.ts are real ports of pieces of it. The loop as
  a whole genuinely isn't ported, so that sentence is half-true — which is the
  awkward kind.

  2. NewVersion/README.md:7 still says "Status: skeleton complete, no gameplay
  classes ported yet." That's plainly false now.

  I'm not confident about the headline "29/557 · 5.2%". It undercounts, but
  arguably not wrongly: EnemyBasic sits at not started while docs/ENEMIES.md
  documents Basic as fully implemented — and both can be true, because the AS3
  EnemyBasic class is a display object whose behaviour actually lives in
  PartGameArea. I don't yet know whether the statuses are stale or the metric
  just measures a different thing. I'd rather say so than pick one.

  What I think the next unit of work is

  The most trustworthy signal isn't PROGRESS.md — it's
  src/game/enemies/enemyBehaviour.ts, an in-code ledger whose numbers are pinned
  by a test: 9 of 20 enemy types implemented, 0 partial, 11 data-only. The 11
  spawn and steer, but nothing that distinguishes them exists. That ledger is
  partly derived (ranged support is computed from
  SUPPORTED_SHOOT_TYPES/SUPPORTED_SHOOT_ANGLES, so it can't drift) and the
  hand-declared half is pinned to real AS3 branches — it's the most honest
  artifact in the repo.

  Grouping those 11 by shared dependency, per CLAUDE.md's rule that blockers get
  named as subsystems and built once:

  - Enemy bullet classes — SUPPORTED_SHOOT_TYPES is only ['Basic','BasicBoss'].
  EnemyBulletFollowing, EnemyBulletHook, EnemyBulletTrap are unported, gating
  Soldier, GrapplingHook and Trap.
  - Per-enemy state across frames — Accelerating, Shrinking, Temperamental,
  DamageAddict.
  - Visibility and steering variants — Ghost, ScaredGhost, Teleporting.
  - Medic — heals nearby enemies; likely standalone.

  My recommendation is to start with EnemyBulletFollowing: it's the smallest
  change with a self-verifying result, because Soldier is data-only only for the
  missing bullet class, and widening SUPPORTED_SHOOT_TYPES makes the derived
  ledger update itself.

  Two caveats I want on the record. I don't yet know whether statusEffects.ts
  already provides the persistent per-enemy timer that CLAUDE.md named as the
  shared blocker — if it does, the second group is cheaper than it looks, and
  I'd check that before committing to an order. And per this project's own
  hardest-won lesson, a green suite is not evidence anything works in the
  running game: I have not launched it, so "9 implemented" means the modules
  exist and their tests pass, not that those enemies behave correctly on screen.

  I haven't modified anything. Want me to start on EnemyBulletFollowing, or
  would you rather I first correct the stale prose in PROGRESS.md and README so
  the map matches the territory?

● Tasks 1–3 done. Report, then I'll move to the visual baseline.

  1. Does statusEffects.ts provide the shared blocker?

  Yes for weapons, and it's wired. No for those four enemy types — and that 
  dissolves the group I proposed.

  The timer exists and is real: statusEffects.ts:9-14 names itself as the thing
  Timed Bomb and Poison Cannon were blocked on. It's wired, not just reachable —
  Enemy.ts:87 gives every enemy a StatusState, Enemy.ts:210 ticks it,
  GameplayScene.ts:1008/:1651 apply bombs and poison. One gap: applyFreeze has
  no non-test caller, because the Ice secondaries that would call it aren't
  built. Ported, not wired.

  But it's a fixed three-field record (poison/bomb/freeze), not a general
  registry, and the four types don't plug into it. What the AS3 says they
  actually need:

  ┌───────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────┐
  │     Type      │               Needs               │         AS3         │
  ├───────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │               │                                   │ spawn :3076,:3085;  │
  │ Accelerating  │ speedTimer/speedTimerMax          │ tick :6697-6715;    │
  │               │                                   │ read :5092          │
  ├───────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │               │                                   │ spawn :3050-3054;   │
  │ Temperamental │ angry/angryTimer/angryTimerMax    │ tick :6640-6667;    │
  │               │                                   │ read :4610,:5094    │
  ├───────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ Shrinking     │ no timer — size = 1/3 + 2/3 *     │ :6772-6777          │
  │               │ (hp/total)                        │                     │
  ├───────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ DamageAddict  │ no timer — damage flipped to      │ :4269, :4893-4917   │
  │               │ healing, speed from HP%           │                     │
  └───────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────┘

  So my grouping was wrong on both halves. Two need no cross-frame state at all
  — they're pure functions of current HP. The other two need one countdown field
  pair each, which is a couple of fields on the enemy record, not a subsystem.
  There is no shared blocker left in that group.

  The ordering consequence: Shrinking and DamageAddict are now the cheapest 
  items on the enemy board, cheaper than EnemyBulletFollowing — no new
  infrastructure, and their inputs already exist. The only genuine shared
  dependency remaining is the enemy bullet classes; the test's own regex
  confirms PartGameArea branches on [object EnemyBulletFollowing], [object 
  EnemyBulletHook] and [object EnemyBulletTrap], so Soldier, GrapplingHook and
  Trap share it. I'll still do EnemyBulletFollowing as you scheduled unless
  you'd rather take the two cheap ones first.

  2. Doc fixes — committed (d390ce0)

  One thing worth knowing: that prose is generated. It's hardcoded in
  scripts/gen-progress.mjs from line 376, so editing PROGRESS.md directly gets
  silently reverted by the next npm run progress — which is exactly what
  progress:check caught me doing. The fix is in the generator; PROGRESS.md is
  regenerated from it. Statuses and the 29/557 headline untouched,
  progress:check green, suite still 1327 passing.

  3. The EnemyBasic / 5.2% question — reporting only, nothing changed

  All 47 Enemy*.as files are [Embed] asset stubs. Not one exceeds 16 lines.
  EnemyBasic.as is 14 lines: import Sprite, [Embed(symbol="symbol1353")], empty
  constructor calling super(). EnemyShrinking (symbol324) and EnemyAccelerating
  (symbol302) are byte-for-byte the same shape. There is no behaviour in any of
  them — it lives entirely in PartGameArea branches keyed on enemyType == "X" or
  [object EnemyX].

  So not started is wrong for the class, and by CLAUDE.md's own rule (not 
  applicable for "[Embed] asset stubs") all 47 belong there.

  The sharper evidence that the metric counts the wrong unit: the board is 
  already inconsistent within this one category. EnemyBulletBasic is marked
  ported · wired and EnemyBasic is not started — the two files are structurally
  identical stubs. Nothing about the class distinguishes them; what differs is
  whether the behaviour got built. The status field is de facto tracking
  behaviour while claiming to track classes.

  Two consequences, and the choice between them is yours because it's a question
  about what the board should measure:

  - Marking the 47 not applicable is what the stated rule says, and would raise
  the percentage by shrinking the denominator — but it deletes the only
  per-enemy tracking on the board, unless enemyBehaviour.ts's 9/0/11 ledger is
  accepted as the replacement. Which is what that module exists for.
  - Leaving them means the Enemies section keeps reading as 8/52 forever while
  enemyBehaviour.ts says 9/20, and the two boards disagree permanently.

  I have not checked whether the other 5 entries in that section (Crown,
  ImageEnemy, MarkerEnemy, WarningEnemy, WarningTimedBomb) are also stubs — I'd
  check before acting on either option.

  The 7 category agents are still sweeping. Three findings are already solid
  from my own lane, and one of them is significant:

  KNIP.md documents a guarantee the tool does not provide. KNIP.md:13-14 states
  "'unused export' here means 'nothing outside a test calls it'", and frames
  knip as the guard against this repo's worst failure mode — isWaveComplete
  ported, tested, and called by nothing for weeks. It does not do that.
  knip.json never disables knip's default vitest plugin, so *.test.ts files are
  entry points and test imports count as consumers. Proof: countMaxedPrimary
  (upgradeState.ts, imported only at upgradeState.test.ts:11) and
  bombFuseRemaining (statusEffects.ts, imported only at statusEffects.test.ts:9)
  are each consumed solely by their own test, and knip flags neither. The 49
  findings it does report are a weaker category — exports with no importer at
  all, mostly module-private helpers that shouldn't be exported. Useful, but not
  the signal the doc promises.

  CLAUDE.md's AS3 citations hold up. Spot-checked the load-bearing ones: :5822,
  :5554, and the :4499–:7044 span. All accurate. No case for touching those
  sections.

  A gap, not a contradiction: CLAUDE.md says "roughly 120 classes fall in this
  bucket [not applicable]"; PROGRESS.md records 5. That's intent the data
  doesn't reflect — related to the 47 Enemy*.as stubs I found earlier.

● Cause established, and it's broader than one enemy. AS3 PartGameArea.as:3318
  sets enemy.radius = enemy.width / 2 — size comes from the embedded art symbol,
  not from any stat column. EnemyTiny is small because its SWF sprite is small.
  The port draws its own circles at a hardcoded BASE_DIAMETER = 26
  (Enemy.ts:32), so all 20 types are identically sized.

  So the finding isn't "Tiny is broken" — it's that per-type size is entirely 
  absent from the port, and Tiny is simply the one type whose whole character is
  that dimension. Yet enemyBehaviour.ts reports it implemented and
  enemyBehaviour.test.ts:130 asserts it('Tiny is complete — it is small, and 
  that is all'). A green test names a property the code does not have.

  once they land.

  ---
  PHASE 1 AUDIT — report only, no writes

  Method. 7 read-only agents across all 13 PROGRESS categories, plus my own
  passes on knip semantics, CLAUDE.md's AS3 citations, and independent
  re-verification of the four biggest claims. verified is unknown for every 
  single unit — nothing here says anything works on screen. Baseline: typecheck
  clean, lint clean, data:check green, 1327 tests passing.

  Critical — these break play, and the suite is green through all of them

  1. All 45 Boss levels auto-win in about half a second, and never spawn 
  anything. First one is level 1-9. (I verified every link myself.)

  GameplayScene.ts:606 calls createWaveState(spec) and omits the second
  argument. waveState.ts:65 defaults bossAmount = 0. Then waveState.ts:269
  returns bossAmountKilled >= bossAmount → 0 >= 0 → complete on frame one, and
  waveState.ts:108 returns canSpawn === false forever. Nothing in src/ computes
  a boss count; the AS3 derives it (ScreenGame.as:703-716) by summing
  composition entries ending in "B", and that data is extracted — level 1-9
  carries { type: "Basic", level: "B", count: 1 } — but nothing reads it.

  Every Boss test passes bossAmount explicitly (flag.test.ts:144,
  waveState.test.ts:83), so the suite is structurally blind. This is
  CLAUDE.md:276-278 verbatim: the ported module was correct, its tests passed, 
  and the defect was in what the scene fed it.

  2. Music can never play. Structurally confirmed above. SoundManager has 17
  real call sites and its SFX path is genuinely wired — but the only music load
  path targets a torn-down scene's loader. Near-miss worth noting:
  112_MusicMenu.mp3 is decoded during preload, under key music-menu
  (manifest.ts:83), while the manager looks for snd:112_MusicMenu.mp3. Two
  naming schemes for one file in one cache. Also: 5 of 8 tracks
  (Normal/Flag/Tower/Defense/Boss) have no call site at all.

  3. Every level plays at the wrong size. LevelSpec.roomWidth/roomHeight are
  extracted for all 405 levels and asserted in tests — and read by no runtime
  code. GameplayScene.ts:113-114 hardcodes 640×960. Level 1-1 should be 640×400.
  This is also the root cause of the dead off-camera spawn search:
  spawnPlacement.ts:70 disables it when roomWidth === cameraWidth, and 640 ===
  640 always, so every enemy in the game enters from a wall and the 25-attempt
  search at :146-155 never runs.

  High — money and progress

  4. Two upgrades are pure money sinks. BulletReflect and KillReload
  (upgradeData.ts:56, :80) are in ALL_UPGRADES, rendered, buyable, persisted —
  and have zero runtime read sites. ~135,000 coins for literally no effect.

  5. Eleven of twelve secondaries are buyable and cannot fire. SECONDARY_WEAPONS
  contains only Mine (secondaries.ts:77). Shield costs up to 12,500
  (upgradeData.ts:358) and UpgradesScene.ts:91 publishes ALL_UPGRADES
  unfiltered.

  6. Dev levels pollute the real save — the opposite of what the doc says.
  devLevels.ts:28-29 claims "playing them cannot pollute a save". The
  recordLevelResult no-op is real, but GameplayScene.ts:1459 banks dev-level
  earnings into the real profile and playerProfile.ts:123-125 writes
  previousWorld: 0 unconditionally. The resume point survives only by a falsy ||
  1 fallback at MainMenuScene.ts:138.

  Exists + tested + NOT wired — the list you expected to be non-empty

  unit: PM_PRNG.ts
  tests: BigInt differential, 35k draws
  why it's dead: zero non-test importers; game uses
  Phaser.Math.RandomDataGenerator (GameplayScene.ts:607,1789); LevelSpec.seed
  never read
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  unit: tutorialState.ts — whole module
  tests: 32
  why it's dead: zero production callers anywhere
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  unit: achievementState.ts — evaluator half
  tests: 40
  why it's dead: evaluate/updateAchievements/wouldWin have no production caller;

  only createInitialStates is reached
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  unit: applyFreeze (statusEffects.ts:169)
  tests: 6
  why it's dead: nothing deals Ice damage; kills 5 downstream branches
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  unit: discoverEnemies + 4 more (enemyKnowledge.ts)
  tests: 7
  why it's dead: bestiary discovery never invoked
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  unit: countMaxed*, getAchievementTiers, getTotalValues
  tests: many
  why it's dead: the achievement data feed — both ends tested against each
  other,
  in tests only
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  unit: keepLoopAlive (SoundManager.ts:202)
  tests: 6
  why it's dead: both loop assets preload, never sound
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  unit: bombFuseRemaining, countCrowd, expireBlastDamage, flagReward, canAfford,

  5 loadout exports, randomEdgeSpawn
  tests: —
  why it's dead: mostly duplicated inline at the call site instead

  Two dead events: ui:pause has a working handler at GameplayScene.ts:396 and no
  emitter — pause is one button away from live. player:healed is handled at
  bridge.ts:73 and never emitted.

  The tooling meant to catch this does not

  knip cannot see any of the above, and KNIP.md says it can. KNIP.md:13-14:
  "'unused export' here means 'nothing outside a test calls it'". knip.json
  never disables the default vitest plugin, so test files are entry points.
  Proof: countMaxedPrimary and bombFuseRemaining are each imported only by their
  own test, and knip flags neither. Its 49 findings are a weaker category —
  exports with no importer at all.

  Two tests pin bugs as specification — CLAUDE.md's own "a test can pin a bug
  and still look like coverage":
  - Hud.test.tsx:148 asserts the readout hides at capacity: 0, commented "Pins 
  the guard that caused the bug above". The guard's reach at Hud.tsx:105 was
  never narrowed — so the fix CLAUDE.md's guard-scoping section prescribes would
  now fail a green test.
  - enemyBehaviour.test.ts:130 asserts it('Tiny is complete — it is small, and 
  that is all'). Tiny is not small. Enemy.ts:134 is isBoss ? BOSS_DIAMETER : 
  BASE_DIAMETER with no type check. Root cause: AS3 PartGameArea.as:3318 sets
  radius = width / 2 — size lives in the art, and the port draws its own
  circles. Per-type size is absent for all 20 types.

  uiEventListeners.test.ts:45 scans only src/ui/screens, so DiagnosticsPanel.tsx
  (in src/ui/) is invisible to the very check whose comment says the
  EnemiesScreen bug shipped "because its pair was simply absent". Its
  audio-selftest button is dead in 6 of 7 scenes.

  GameplayScene.ts is 69 KB with no behavioural test. killTally.test.ts
  readFileSyncs it and regex-matches source text.

  Doc contradictions — the ones that matter

  - PROGRESS says ScreenGame and PartGameArea are "not started"; both run the
  frame loop. README:7 says the opposite. PROGRESS also contradicts itself —
  :206/:214 say "ported · wired" while :814-822 says "not ported".
  - docs/ENEMIES.md:114 states Exploding has no AS3 branch. It has two.
  Generator bug at gen-enemy-reference.ts:227-229 conflates "no unported
  mechanic" with "no branch" — in the file README calls drift-proof.
  - CLAUDE.md:237-239 — the per-enemy status timer as a live blocker. Disproved;
  both weapons shipped.
  - CLAUDE.md:187-189 — PM_PRNG "drives deterministic background-prop
  placement". It drives nothing.
  - CLAUDE.md:63-68 — bridge is the "sole" translator. safeArea.ts:87 and
  errorCapture.ts:36 call setters directly.
  - CLAUDE.md:113 / README:74 — scene chain omits UpgradesScene and
  EnemiesScene.
  - CLAUDE.md's AS3 citations are all accurate (:5822, :5554, :4499-7044). The
  pitfall sections are well-sourced and need no edits.

  On the 5.2% metric: 472 of 562 AS3 classes are [Embed] asset stubs (≤16
  lines). Only 90 contain logic. The board counts stubs as portable units, and
  inconsistently — EnemyBulletBasic is "ported · wired" while EnemyBasic is "not
  started", though both files are identical stubs.

  What I could not establish

  Everything above is static analysis. verified is unknown for all of it. The
  two I'd most want your eyes on: does menu music play on first tap, and what 
  actually happens on level 1-9.

● Steps 0–2 done and pushed, plus knip. Five commits on develop.

  Step 0 — audit recorded, four CLAUDE.md claims fixed (72cc30a)

  NewVersion/docs/AUDIT-2026-07.md, with the held dead-module list you asked
  for. CLAUDE.md got exactly four hunks — status timer, PM_PRNG, "sole
  translator", scene chain. The status-timer edit turns it past-tense and notes
  the grouping heuristic held, since statusEffects.ts did unblock both weapons
  at once. Pitfall sections untouched; I'd spot-checked their AS3 citations
  (:5822, :5554, :4499-7044) and all were accurate.

  Step 1 — Boss levels (8001772)

  bossCountFor() ports getBossCount including the mode guard at
  ScreenGame.as:371-377, which matters because that zero is what canSpawn uses
  as its Boss-only gate.

  One deviation from your instruction, deliberately. You said feed it at
  GameplayScene.ts:606. I made it the default for createWaveState's parameter
  instead. Two reasons: a caller cannot forget a value it never has to pass, so
  the omission cannot recur; and it's the only version that's testable — the
  scene can't be instantiated, so a test can only reach this by calling
  createWaveState(spec) the way the scene does. Passing it explicitly at the
  call site would have left the seam untestable.

  The test, and its failure on old code. New block in waveState.test.ts that
  builds level 1-9 with one argument. Reverting only the default to 0: 4 of 7 
  fail, with the audited symptoms — expected +0 to be 1, complete-on-frame-one
  true instead of false, canSpawn false instead of true, and completion without
  a kill. The 3 that pass guard the fixture and the pure function, which were
  never the problem. I also dropped the hand-supplied bossAmount from the two
  flag.test.ts Boss tests, since that pattern is what blinded the suite.

  Step 2 — room size (9a25f25)

  resolveLevelSpec() now runs first in create(); the old constants survive only
  as FALLBACK_ROOM. The ordering was the bug — startWave() resolved the spec
  after everything that needs the room's extent.

  Your question: it revives the search, and spawnPlacement.ts needs no fix. I
  checked its guard against PartGameArea.as:7245 — same five disqualifiers in
  the same order, faithful port; only the input was wrong. The search now runs
  on 195 of 405 levels (Normal 75, Flag 75, Boss 45). The other 210 are excluded
  by the original's own rule: 640-wide rooms have no horizontal off-camera
  space, and Defense is disqualified outright.

  Tests in two halves — behavioural (five real room sizes; off-camera reachable
  at 900×720, impossible at 640×960) and a source-shape seam test following
  killTally.test.ts's precedent, since the scene needs a live Phaser game.
  Stashing the pre-fix scene: all 4 seam tests fail, the behavioural 7 pass —
  the right split.

  knip (419b5cc)

  "vitest": false alone did nothing; the Vite plugin also injects test entries
  from the test block in vite.config.ts. Needed both plugins off, an explicit
  entry, and test files ignored. 49 → 242, and PM_PRNG.ts now surfaces as an
  unused file. It independently reproduces nearly the whole audit dead-list.

  Triaged in KNIP.md, not acted on. The genuinely new group is small: helpers

  profile and                                                                   
      playerProfile.ts:123-125 writes previousWorld: 0 unconditionally, while   
      devLevels.ts:28-29 claims the opposite. Either fix that or give dev mode  
  its own                                                                       
      profile slot. Whichever you choose, the doc comment must end up true.     
    - Commit it on develop as a real feature, not a stash-it-later hack. I'll   
  be using                                                                      
      this for every visual pass from here on.                                  
                                                                                
  1. Why does createWaveState's bossAmount parameter still exist? Making the    
  computed                                                                      
     value the default is stronger than what I asked for and I'm keeping it.    
  But if                                                                        
     it's a pure function of the spec, an overridable parameter is a loaded gun 
  — a                                                                           
     future test can pass its own value and re-blind the suite exactly the way  
     flag.test.ts did. If a real caller needs to override, name it. If none     
  does,                                                                         
     delete the parameter.                                                      
                                                                                
  2. Your room-size seam test follows killTally.test.ts's precedent — which the 
  audit                                                                         
     itself flagged as a weakness: a 69 KB scene guarded by regex over source   
  text. I                                                                       
     accept it as the best available today, but record it in the audit doc as a 
  known                                                                         
     limit, not as coverage. A source-shape test passes when the source looks   
  right                                                                         
     and says nothing about behaviour.                                          
                                                                                
  3. The knip category you called "genuinely new" is the most dangerous finding 
  in this                                                                       
     pass and you understated it. countCrowd / canAfford / flagReward: two      
  copies of                                                                     
     one rule, the tested copy unused, the live copy inlined at the call site.  
  That is                                                                       
     worse than dead code — dead code doesn't lie, but here a green test guards 
  the                                                                           
     wrong instance and the two can diverge silently. Give it its own section   
  in the                                                                        
     audit doc, above the plain dead-module list.                               
                                                                                
  Then I'll do the visual pass.

      141  
      142  ---
      143  
      144 +## One rule, two copies — the tested one unused               
      145 +                                                              
      146 +**Read this before the dead-module list below.** It is a worse
          + failure than dead                                            
      147 +code, and it is easy to mistake for it.                       
      148 +                                                              
      149 +In each case a helper exists, is exported, and has real tests.
          + The caller does not                                          
      150 +call it — it reimplements the same rule inline. So there are t
          +wo copies of one                                              
      151 +rule, a green test guards the copy that never runs, and the co
          +py that *does* run                                            
      152 +is unguarded. Dead code is inert and honest. This actively mis
          +leads: the suite                                              
      153 +reports the behaviour as covered, and the two copies can drift
          + apart silently, at                                           
      154 +which point the tests keep passing while the game does somethi
          +ng else.                                                      
      155 +                                                              
      156 +| Rule | Tested copy | Live copy |                            
      157 +|---|---|---|                                                 
      158 +| Crowd count within a flame radius | `weapons/flames.ts:120` 
          +`countCrowd`, 3 tests | `GameplayScene.ts:938-943`, same `Math
          +.hypot(...) < FLAME_CROWD_RADIUS` loop written out |          
      159 +| Whether the player can afford an upgrade | `upgrades/upgrade
          +State.ts:86` `canAfford` | `UpgradesScene.ts:101`, `cost !== n
          +ull && state.money >= cost` |                                 
      160 +| Flag payout | `waves/flag.ts:155` `flagReward` | `GameplaySc
          +ene.ts:1386`, `spec.flagMoney` inlined |                      
      161 +                                                              
      162 +None is currently divergent — checked at the time of the audit
          +. That is the point:                                          
      163 +they agree *now*, nothing enforces that they keep agreeing, an
          +d the mechanism that                                          
      164 +would normally catch it (a failing test) is pointed at the wro
          +ng instance.                                                  
      165 +                                                              
      166 +`npm run knip` surfaces these as unused exports, which reads a
          +s "safe to delete" —                                          
      167 +the opposite of the correct action. Deleting the tested copy l
          +eaves the live copy                                           
      168 +untested; the fix is to call it.

● Blocking item done and pushed (d3c02cd), plus your three notes.

  The factual question first

  Neither description was right. LevelSelectScene.ts:24 sets SELECTED_WORLD = 1,
  so only world 1 is published — all 45 levels, not pinned to level 1. My "pins
  world 1" was accurate but missed the thing that actually blocks you: :94,
  unlocked: level === 1 || isLevelCleared(progress, world, level - 1). Reaching 
  1-9 needs eight clears first. The sequential lock was the blocker, not the
  world pin.

  The dev jump

  On the level select screen, below the real grid: world buttons 1–9, then all
  45 levels of that world, each labelled with its mode so Boss and Flag are
  findable at a glance. It emits the same ui:start-game the real grid does — no
  second route into GameplayScene — and sits behind import.meta.env.DEV at its
  only call site, matching the EnemiesScreen pattern. I ran a production build
  and confirmed it's absent from the bundle rather than assuming Vite would
  strip it.

  Save safety — I chose the guard, not a separate slot. A sandbox flag on
  ui:start-game gates the whole persistence block in GameplayScene, so money,
  the level result and "where the player was" stay out together rather than the
  profile ending up half-updated. One guard, one place, scoped to the rule. It's
  forwarded by all three menu scenes, survives a retry, and is inherited across
  "Next level" — the Hud has no way to know a run's provenance, and a chain
  entered from a dev jump wasn't legitimately reached either.

  That also makes two lying comments true: devLevels.ts:28-29 and
  EnemiesScreen's "It records no progress". The world-0 sentinel really did
  no-op recordLevelResult — it just never covered the two writes that mattered,
  and covers the jump not at all, since that uses real world numbers. The
  comment now says what actually provides the guarantee.

  1. bossAmount parameter — deleted

  You were right, and I checked before deciding: no real caller overrode it. The
  only three were tests, and all three were constructing states the game cannot
  produce — a Normal-mode spec with B entries, state.mode = 'Boss' set
  afterwards, and a hand-picked quota unrelated to the composition (count 3,
  quota 1). They now build Boss-mode specs and let the quota derive. Parameter
  gone; the docstring says to add a named entry point if a real caller ever
  needs one, rather than restoring it.

  2. Source-shape tests — recorded as a limit

  In the audit's coverage-holes section, naming all three uses. The wording is
  blunt about what it can't see: a guard that's present but never reached, an
  argument forwarded but ignored, an ordering right in the file and wrong at
  runtime — the exact class of defect this audit found three of. Noted that
  every rule guarded this way has no behavioural coverage, and the real fix is
  making the scene testable.

  3. One rule, two copies — promoted

  Its own section above the dead-module list. You're right that I understated
  it, and the sharpest part is one I'd missed: knip's output reads backwards 
  here. "Unused export" means "safe to delete", and deleting removes the tested
  copy while leaving the untested live one running. Flagged in KNIP.md as the
  one category to treat first and never to fix by deletion. None of the three is
  currently divergent — that's the point: they agree now, nothing enforces it,
  and the mechanism that would catch drift is aimed at the wrong instance.

  ---
  64 files, 1358 tests, typecheck and lint clean, knip steady at 242. Server is
  live on http://localhost:5173/ and serving the new code.

  For the visual pass — the dev jump is under the level grid. World 1 → level 9
  is the Boss fix; 1-1 vs 1-2 is the room-size fix (640×400 vs 900×720, should
  look clearly different now). Everything launched from the jump is sandboxed,
  so none of it touches your save.

● Both done and pushed (3b92fef). 65 files, 1369 tests, typecheck/lint clean,
  dev jump confirmed absent from a production build.

  1. Yes — it has a behavioural test now

  You were right, and the criticism lands harder than you put it: I wrote that
  source-shape tests can't see "an argument forwarded but ignored" and then
  guarded exactly that with source-shape tests.

  I extracted the level-end persistence into player/levelBanking.ts so it can be
  driven directly, and levelBanking.test.ts runs a real PlayerProfile over a 
  real SaveStore, comparing the encoded save string byte for byte. It observes
  rather than infers: a sandbox run leaves an existing save identical, invents
  no progress in an unreached world, doesn't bank a loss, and leaves the
  in-memory profile alone so a later real save can't flush it.

  Confirmed by deleting the guard — 5 of 7 fail with real diffs: money 999999 vs
  500, previousWorld 9 vs 1, and the save strings themselves differing. Not a
  shape mismatch.

  The two source-shape assertions that described the old inline block are
  replaced, not restored. What's left in sandboxRun.test.ts is only what lives
  in the scene and nowhere else.

  2. The boss is killable, but you'd have been right to distrust it

  At base stats: 500 HP boss, Cannon at 7 damage / 13-frame reload = 72 hits = 
  31.2 s of perfect uninterrupted fire, while 19 escorts carrying 95 total
  contact damage converge on your 100 HP tank. "Boss doesn't die" is the natural
  and wrong conclusion.

  So the dev jump now has an "arrive fully upgraded" toggle, on by default. It's
  gated on sandbox and equipped — that pairing is the safety property, since
  the only thing keeping a maxed profile out of your save is that a sandbox run
  never banks. There's a test asserting equipped is never the sole condition.

  Equipped time-to-kill on 1-9: MiniGun 6.4 s · Timed Bomb 9.1 s · Cannon 18.3 s
  · Cake 18.7 s. Press Q to cycle. Shotgun (88.7 s) and Poison (72.8 s) are
  worse than the starting Cannon against one high-HP target — don't read those
  as broken.

  Verification procedure — check it, don't trust it

  Your save is one localStorage key: CircularTankSave1.

  Before jumping, in DevTools console:
  const before = localStorage.getItem('CircularTankSave1');
  copy(before);            // also puts it on your clipboard
  before?.length            // note this number

  Then do the whole dev pass — jump to 1-9, win or lose it, retry, Next level,
  hit M for coins, play the enemy Test levels. As destructive as you like.

  After, same console:
  localStorage.getItem('CircularTankSave1') === before   // must be true

  true means byte-identical — no money, no result, no previousWorld. If it's
  ever false, that's a real bug and the diff tells us which write escaped:
  JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem('CircularTankSave1'))
  The saveString field holds the encoded slot; m= is money, las= the progress
  table.

  One caveat I want stated plainly: this covers levels launched from the dev
  jump, the enemy Test buttons, and the all-enemy level. It does not cover the
  shop — buying an upgrade calls profile.save() directly (UpgradesScene.ts:147)
  and that's a real purchase. If you want the save provably untouched, don't buy
  anything during the pass. The M key only adds to the in-run counter, so that
  one is safe.

  Ready when you are: 1-9 for Boss, 1-1 vs 1-2 for room size, menu music, Q
  mid-level, and Enemies → Shooting → Test.

● The trace closes it, but you asked me to prove it, so let me write the test
  for that exact scenario rather than assert it.

  Committed a7974da, pushed to develop, ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● 1. The seam — traced, and the hazard is not real

  Where the grant lives: GameplayScene.init assigns this.upgrades = 
  maxedUpgradeState(...) — a field on the scene, pointing at a freshly built
  object. It never touches PlayerProfile.

  Two things close it, and both are true by construction rather than by intent,
  which is why they needed pinning:

  - maxedUpgradeState() builds new arrays via .map(), so the grant is detached.
  The profile's data.upgrades is never replaced.
  - Both shop writes read profile.upgrades, not the scene's copy —
  UpgradesScene.ts:145 does purchaseNextLevel(profile.upgrades, spec). A
  purchase persists the real profile plus that purchase.

  Committed two tests rather than telling you it's fine (a7974da): one mutates
  the grant as hard as possible and asserts the profile is unchanged; the other
  runs the full scenario — real profile → equipped sandbox run → shop purchase —
  and asserts what lands on disk is the real upgrades plus that one purchase,
  nothing maxed, none of the run's fortune.

  One asymmetry worth knowing, because it reads backwards. In a non-equipped
  run, this.upgrades aliases the profile's live object — playerProfile.ts
  returns it directly, which the audit flagged as latent. Nothing mutates it
  today (GameplayScene only reads .money off it), but equipped mode is the safer
  of the two, not the riskier one.

  So my earlier advice was over-cautious in one direction and under-cautious in 
  the other. You can visit the shop — it won't leak the grant. But a purchase is
  a real purchase and spends real money, so if you want a byte-identical save,
  still don't buy. That's a spend, not a leak.

  2. What spread is normal

  Treat my table as a floor, not a prediction. Every number assumes perfect aim,
  zero travel time, and no escorts. Real sources of divergence, roughly in
  order of size:

  - Missed shots — the boss moves at 1.5 units/frame and bullets are
  projectiles, not hitscan (except Laser). At range, lead error dominates.
  - Escorts eating rounds — 19 of them, 10 HP each, between you and the boss.
  Shots spent on them aren't hitting it.
  - Time-of-flight — the gap between firing and the hit registering, per shot.
  - Frame pacing — reload is in AS3 frames converted from deltaMs, so it should
  be rate-independent, but MiniGun at a 1-frame reload is the most sensitive to
  that of any weapon.

  Normal: 1.5–3× the floor. MiniGun at 10–20 s, Timed Bomb at 15–30 s, Cannon at
  30–60 s — all unremarkable.

  Worth reporting as a finding:

  - >5× floor (MiniGun past ~30 s) with shots visibly connecting — suggests
  damage isn't applying per bullet.
  - Enemies don't flash on hit. Enemy.flashDamage is wired to the damage path,
  so a hit with no flash means the hit isn't registering at all. This is your
  best per-shot signal — there's no enemy HP bar.
  - Boss flashes but never dies — damage applying but not accumulating, or death
  not triggering at 0 HP.
  - Boss dies but the level doesn't end — that's bossAmountKilled not
  incrementing, i.e. my fix half-landed.
  - MiniGun no faster than Cannon — 2.6 dmg @ 1 frame vs 10 @ 11 frames is a ~3×
  DPS gap; if they feel equal, the reload clock isn't being honoured.

  Those last two are the ones that would actually indict this session's work.
  The rest is tuning.

  Snapshot first — copy(localStorage.getItem('CircularTankSave1')) — then 1-9,
  1-1 vs 1-2, menu music, Q, and Enemies → Shooting → Test. Server's still live
  on 5173.

● Now demonstrating the failure on old code — reverting the call site to what it

  Committed 561f62a, pushed to develop, ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Found it, and you were right that reading was the wrong instrument — though
  not in the way either of us guessed. Pushed as 561f62a.

  1. What :7245 compares — the operands

  as3
  camRoomDifX = roomWidth - cameraWidth;
  camRoomDifY = roomHeight - cameraHeight;
  if(!(xPos > camRoomDifX/2 && xPos < roomWidth - camRoomDifX/2
    && yPos > camRoomDifY/2 && yPos < roomHeight - camRoomDifY/2))

  The rectangle is cameraWidth × cameraHeight, centred in the room — roomWidth -
  2*(camRoomDifX/2) is exactly cameraWidth. Coordinate space is room
  coordinates.

  It does not account for scroll. The camera's position is never read;
  camRoomDifX is the scroll range, and half of it is the inset from each side.
  So this is the intersection of every possible camera position — the region on
  screen no matter where the camera has gone.

  Two consequences, both faithful: a point that passes is only guaranteed
  outside that always-visible core, so at one camera extreme it can still be on
  screen — the original does this too. And your guess was close but inverted:
  the AS3 compares against camera size, not its on-screen rect.

  The port's predicate is that expression term for term. marginX is
  camRoomDifX/2. It was faithful. The operand wasn't. The AS3's camera was a
  fixed 640×400, so a constant was correct there. This port's camera is 640 ×
  logicalHeight = renderHeight / zoom, clamped to [400, 1440] — 400 is the 
  floor, hit only on a very wide window. Feeding 400 while rendering 900
  protects a rectangle 320 units shorter than the real view. Measured: >20% of 
  accepted placements at 900×720 in a 640×900 viewport land inside the actual
  view.

  2. Positions outside the room

  The search never produces one — candidates are random() * roomWidth, always
  inside. Only the edge fallback reaches the boundary, and only spawnEnemy
  pushes past it (:3298-3313): wall==1 → enemy.y -= height/2, wall==2 → enemy.x 
  -= width/2, and so on. So the outward inset you remember is real, and it
  belongs to edge spawns.

  There was no clamp to remove. Your suspicion was reasonable but the port
  doesn't clamp here.

  The fix — operand, not nudge

  GameplayScene passes the live viewport. CAMERA_WIDTH/CAMERA_HEIGHT became
  AS3_CAMERA_*, reference only, and cameraWidth/cameraHeight are now required on
  PlacementContext — omitting them is a compile error, not a silent 400.
  Confirmed by reverting the call site: it no longer typechecks. That's a
  stronger guard than a test, and it's the same move as deleting the bossAmount
  parameter.

  Also widened roomWidth == cameraWidth to <=, since a dynamic camera height
  routinely exceeds the room where equality is false but there's still nowhere
  to hide. Identical behaviour on every room size the AS3 could produce.

  The test problem — you were right

  .offCamera === true checks the search found a point, not that the point is out
  of view. Green while placement was wrong. Deleted, not weakened.

  offCameraSpawn.test.ts asserts the real property over 400 placements across
  six room/viewport combinations: outside the visible core, inside the room,
  wall === 0. It also guards against a vacuous pass — if the search never
  succeeds, nothing was asserted, so that's a failure too.

  One honest caveat on "confirm it fails on current code": the module was
  already correct given correct input, so a module-level test can't fail — which
  is the whole trap. The failure I can demonstrate is at the seam: the old call
  site doesn't compile, and the measurement case quantifies what it was doing.

  66 files, 1375 tests, typecheck and lint clean. Worth re-checking on 1-2 or
  1-4 — and note enemies will still sometimes enter within view at a camera
  extreme, because that's what the original does.

  You justified widening == to <= with "identical behaviour on every room size  
  the                                                                           
  AS3 could produce." But that set is precisely where it doesn't matter. The    
  defect                                                                        
  you just found existed because this port's camera domain is larger than the   
  original's — fixed 640×400 there, dynamic [400,1440] here. Justifying a       
  change by                                                                     
  reference to the AS3's world, when the port lives in a wider one, is the same 
  reasoning error one level up.                                                 
                                                                                
  The concrete consequence: whether the off-camera search runs at all now       
  depends on                                                                    
  the player's live viewport, not on the level. In the AS3 that was a static    
  property                                                                      
  of the level because the camera never changed. Two things follow:

  longer a                                                                      
     constant. It was computed against the fixed 400. Recompute it as a         
  function of

  logical                                                                       
     viewport, at 640×400, and at 640×1440? If that count swings widely, spawn

  needs

     its camera been dynamic) or a deliberate divergence. Either is acceptable.

● Decisive, and it shows my change was a regression, not just a badly-justified
  one.

  With <=: at any viewport ≥720 logical height the search is enabled on zero 
  levels. On a phone (logicalHeight ≈ 1385) enemies would always enter from
  edges — the feature disappears entirely on mobile.

  The acceptance-rate row explains why: a 900-wide room in a 640-wide camera has
  28.9% of candidates still valid at any height, because those are the
  horizontally off-camera points. Height can't eliminate them. Reverting.

      141  
      142  ---
      143  
      144 +## Fixed-camera assumptions in a dynamic-camera port          
      145 +                                                              
      146 +A recurring class, worth its own heading because it has now ca
          +used one shipped                                              
      147 +bug and one shipped regression, and both looked faithful when 
          +read.                                                         
      148 +                                                              
      149 +The AS3's camera was **a fixed 640×400 Flash stage**. This por
          +t's is                                                        
      150 +**640 × `logicalHeight`**, where `logicalHeight` is `renderHei
          +ght / zoom` clamped                                           
      151 +to `[400, 1440]` (`config/viewport.ts`). Any AS3 expression in
          +volving the camera                                            
      152 +is therefore a *constant* there and a *variable* here, and por
          +ting it term for                                              
      153 +term preserves the arithmetic while silently changing what it 
          +means.                                                        
      154 +                                                              
      155 +Two instances so far:                                         
      156 +                                                              
      157 +1. **The off-camera spawn search compared against 640×400.** F
          +aithful predicate,                                            
      158 +   wrong operand — the protected rectangle was up to 320 units
          + shorter than the                                             
      159 +   real viewport, so >20% of "off-camera" placements were on s
          +creen. Fixed by                                               
      160 +   requiring the live viewport on `PlacementContext`; omitting
          + it is now a                                                  
      161 +   compile error.                                             
      162 +2. **The `roomWidth == cameraWidth || roomHeight == cameraHeig
          +ht` disqualifier                                              
      163 +   was widened to `<=`**, justified as "identical on every roo
          +m size the AS3                                                
      164 +   could produce". That justification reasons in the AS3's wor
          +ld about a change                                             
      165 +   that only matters in this one. It disabled the search on **
          +all 405 levels at                                             
      166 +   any logical height ≥ 720** — i.e. everywhere on a phone. Re
          +verted to `==`.                                               
      167 +   The two axes are independent: a 900-wide room in a 640-wide
          + camera keeps                                                 
      168 +   28.9% of candidates valid at any height, and the "camera co
          +vers this                                                     
      169 +   dimension" case needs no guard because a negative margin wi
          +dens that band                                                
      170 +   past the room and the test falls through to the other axis 
          +on its own.                                                   
      171 +                                                              
      172 +**Known divergence, deliberate.** With `==` restored the struc
          +ture is faithful,                                             
      173 +but two properties are now viewport-dependent where the origin
          +al's were fixed:                                              
      174 +                                                              
      175 +- **The acceptance rate varies.** In a 900×720 room, 60.5% of 
          +candidates are                                                
      176 +  accepted at a 640×400 viewport and 28.9% at 640×720 or talle
          +r. A taller window                                            
      177 +  genuinely shows more of the room, so fewer positions are off
          + screen. This is                                              
      178 +  the honest physical consequence, not a defect.              
      179 +- **The height-equality disqualifier is effectively dead.** It
          + fires only when                                              
      180 +  `logicalHeight` exactly equals a room height — a measure-zer
          +o coincidence with                                            
      181 +  a continuous viewport, where in the AS3 it was a static prop
          +erty of a level. A                                            
      182 +  one-pixel resize flips it. Pinned in `offCameraSpawn.test.ts
          +` so the behaviour                                            
      183 +  is recorded rather than discovered.                         
      184 +                                                              
      185 +**Before porting any other camera-derived expression, ask whet
          +her the AS3 value                                             
      186 +was constant.** If it was, the port needs the live value and p
          +robably needs it                                              
      187 +to be non-optional.                                           
      188 +

● Reverted and documented (af0ffaf). 1381 tests green. Now the music groundwork

  Ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● You were right twice, and the second one is worse than you framed it — my <=
  widening wasn't a badly-justified harmless change, it was a regression I 
  shipped. Reverted in af0ffaf.

  1. The count is not a constant, and the swing is total

  ┌───────────────┬─────────────────┬─────────────────┐
  │ logicalHeight │ enabled with == │ enabled with <= │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ 400           │ 195             │ 195             │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ 600           │ 120             │ 120             │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ 720           │ 75              │ 0               │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ 900           │ 195             │ 0               │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ 1440          │ 195             │ 0               │
  └───────────────┴─────────────────┴─────────────────┘

  At any logical height ≥ 720, <= disabled the search on all 405 levels. A
  portrait phone is ≈1385, so the feature would have vanished on the platform
  this port targets — and only on mobile, so a desktop pass would never show it.

  What I missed: the two axes are independent. A 900-wide room in a 640-wide
  camera has off-camera space left and right however tall the window is — 28.9% 
  of candidates stay valid at any height. And the "camera covers this dimension"
  case needs no guard at all: it makes that margin negative, which widens the
  band past the room, so the test falls through to the other axis by itself. The
  arithmetic already did what my guard was trying to do.

  New test pins five viewport heights and asserts each acceptance is
  horizontally outside the band — it fails five ways against <=.

  2. Faithful, with two stated divergences

  Structure: faithful. With == restored the expression matches the AS3 term for
  term.

  Divergence, deliberate and now documented under a new audit heading,
  "Fixed-camera assumptions in a dynamic-camera port":

  - The acceptance rate is viewport-dependent — 60.5% at 640×400, 28.9% at
  640×720+. A taller window genuinely shows more room, so fewer positions are
  off screen. Honest physical consequence.
  - The height-equality disqualifier is effectively dead. It fires only when
  logicalHeight exactly equals a room height — measure-zero with a continuous
  viewport, where in the AS3 it was a static property of a level. A one-pixel
  resize flips it. Pinned in a test so it's recorded, not discovered.

  The heading also carries the general rule: before porting any camera-derived 
  expression, ask whether the AS3 value was constant. If it was, the port needs
  the live value, and probably needs it non-optional.

  Menu music — groundwork, not fixed

  The full set is five defects, not one:

  1. Dead loader scene — backend bound to PreloadScene, which scene.start()s
  away seven lines later.
  2. Two key schemes for one file — 112_MusicMenu.mp3 is decoded as music-menu
  (manifest.ts:83) while the manager looks for snd:112_MusicMenu.mp3. The bytes
  are already in the cache and invisible.
  3. Wedged in-flight guard — loadInFlight.add(key) is cleared only by listeners
  on the dead loader, so every later request early-returns.
  4. 5 of 8 tracks have no call site — Normal, Flag, Tower, Defense, Boss are
  never requested. Per-level-mode music isn't wired at all.
  5. applyVolumes() is never called — so even a working toggle wouldn't take
  effect.

  On the mute control: the original had one, and almost all of it is already 
  ported and unwired. SoundManager.as:14-22 has
  soundOn/musicOn/soundVol/musicVol; SaveManager.as:846-863 persists all four —
  to a separate SharedObject, CircularTankOptions, not the save-string slot.
  That's exactly SaveStore.ts:27 OPTIONS_STORE, which the audit already lists as
  ported, tested, and wired to nothing. The toggle lived in PartInterface
  (bToggleSound), i.e. the in-game HUD. So this is a port with its own save 
  field, not a divergence — and mostly a wiring job.

  On the clicking — yes, expect it. Don't report it. All 123 of 123 files lack a
  Xing/LAME header. The audit's own words: Web Audio decodes the codec's
  529-sample delay as ~12 ms of leading silence and may click on loop. The
  tracks are long enough that you'll hear it as a distinct gap rather than a
  stutter: Menu 86 s, Defense 74 s, Normal 67 s, Boss 64 s. Same for
  sndFlameThrowerLoop and sndBurningLoop.

  No reason it would sound clean. The only fix is re-encoding to OGG/AAC —
  docs/AUDIO_PIPELINE.md already calls for it. A click on loop is a known,
  recorded asset problem, not a regression in whatever wiring lands.

  1381 tests green. Ready for your re-check of 1-2 and 1-4 — and yes, expect
  some in-view entries at camera extremes.

      203  `levelDataModelW1`, `PartGameArea.cameraWidth`, …). The level

      205  
      206 +### An AS3 constant that became a runtime variable            
      207 +                                                              
      208 +The Flash build ran at one fixed size on one fixed platform. T
          +his port does not,                                            
      209 +and **any AS3 value that was a compile-time constant but is a 
          +runtime variable here                                         
      210 +will port term-for-term while silently changing meaning**. The
          + arithmetic survives;                                         
      211 +the semantics do not. Camera size is the instance that has bit
          +ten twice, but the                                            
      212 +class is wider — frame rate, stage size, pixel density and saf
          +e-area insets are all                                         
      213 +constants-that-became-variables.                              
      214 +                                                              
      215 +Both instances so far involved `cameraWidth`/`cameraHeight`, a
          + fixed 640×400 in the                                         
      216 +AS3 and `640 × logicalHeight` here, where `logicalHeight` is `
          +renderHeight / zoom`                                          
      217 +clamped to `[400, 1440]`:                                     
      218 +                                                              
      219 +- The off-camera spawn search compared candidates against 640×
          +400 while the game                                            
      220 +  rendered up to 1440 tall, so it protected a rectangle less t
          +han half the height of                                        
      221 +  the real view and placed enemies on screen. The *predicate* 
          +was a faithful port;                                          
      222 +  the *operand* was not.                                      
      223 +- The fix then over-corrected: a `==` disqualifier was widened
          + to `<=` and justified                                        
      224 +  as "identical on every room size the AS3 could produce" — re
          +asoning in the AS3's                                          
      225 +  world about a change that only matters in this one. It disab
          +led the feature on                                            
      226 +  every level at any height ≥ 720.                            
      227 +                                                              
      228 +**The platform-blindness consequence is the important part.** 
          +That second defect was                                        
      229 +*invisible at desktop viewports and total at phone ones*: a wi
          +de window gives a                                             
      230 +logical height near the 400 floor, where everything looked cor
          +rect, while a portrait                                        
      231 +phone sits near 1385, where the feature was entirely gone. Thi
          +s port ships to                                               
      232 +iOS/Android via Capacitor. **A desktop-only visual pass proves
          + nothing about mobile**,                                      
      233 +and neither defect was found by tests or by play — one surface
          +d because a reviewer                                          
      234 +questioned a justification.                                   
      235 +                                                              
      236 +This is a different failure class from the wiring bugs above. 
          +There the code was                                            
      237 +wrong everywhere and nobody had looked. Here the code is *corr
          +ect on the platform you                                       
      238 +test on* and absent on the platform you ship to, so looking ha
          +rder in the same place                                        
      239 +never finds it.                                               
      240 +                                                              
      241 +Before porting any expression involving a screen, camera, view
          +port or timing value:                                         
      242 +                                                              
      243 +1. **Ask whether the AS3 value was constant.** If it was, the 
          +port almost certainly                                         
      244 +   needs the live value, not the transcribed number.          
      245 +2. **Make the live value non-optional.** `PlacementContext` no
          +w requires                                                    
      246 +   `cameraWidth`/`cameraHeight`; omitting them is a compile er
          +ror rather than a                                             
      247 +   silent fallback to the Flash stage. A default that happens 
          +to be right on your                                           
      248 +   machine is the trap.                                       
      249 +3. **Check the behaviour at both extremes of the range**, not 
          +at the value your                                             
      250 +   window happens to produce. For the viewport that means a lo
          +gical height of 400                                           
      251 +   *and* 1440.                                                
      252 +4. **Keep the AS3 constant as documentation, never as a fallba
          +ck.**                                                         
      253 +   `AS3_CAMERA_WIDTH`/`AS3_CAMERA_HEIGHT` exist to record what
          + the original was, and                                        
      254 +   nothing reads them at runtime.

● CLAUDE.md generalised and pushed (0682748). Here's the sweep — read-only,
  nothing changed.

  Sweep: AS3 constants that became runtime variables

  I checked every read of a camera, viewport, zoom, or safe-area value in
  src/game/, src/state/, src/ui/, and traced each back to its AS3 counterpart.

  One correction to my own method first. I initially concluded the AS3 laser had
  no on-screen gate, because grep checkWithinScreen found only 4 uses and none
  was the laser. Wrong — at PartGameArea.as:5566 the same expression is inlined 
  rather than called. That's CLAUDE.md's alias trap in a new costume: identical
  logic living under a name and copy-pasted, so grepping the name undercounts.
  I'd have reported a false finding if I'd stopped there.

  Instance 3 and 4 — found on purpose

  GameplayScene.ts:893 (laser hits) and :955 (magic retargeting) both gate on
  this.cameras.main.worldView.contains(...).

  The AS3 equivalents — checkWithinScreen (:4329-4336), inlined at :5566 for the
  laser and called at :1720 for magic — compute:

  as3
  !(x < 0 - w/2 - cameraPosX || x > roomWidth + w/2 - cameraPosX - (roomWidth -
  cameraWidth) || ...)

  Reduce it and the rect is (scrollX − w/2, scrollX + cameraWidth + w/2).
  Position varies (cameraPosX); size is the constant 640×400. The port's
  worldView has variable position and variable size.

  Consequence: vertical reach for both weapons scales with the player's window,
  bounded by room height. On a 900×720 room the AS3 allowed 400 units of
  vertical reach; the port allows up to 720 — 1.8×. On a 640×960 room, up to
  2.4×. A phone player gets materially longer effective laser and magic range
  than a desktop player, on identical levels. Same class as the spawn bug, same
  direction, not yet observed because nobody has played on a phone.

  I'm not fixing these pending your call, because unlike the spawn search
  there's a real question: worldView is arguably "more correct" (what's on
  screen is on screen), but it makes weapon reach hardware-dependent. Faithful
  would be a fixed 640×400 rect at the live scroll position.

  Cleared — checked and not affected

  Committed 31e5a20, pushed to develop (ctrl+o to expand)

● Both filed (31e5a20). Two reports, and the first reverses my recommendation.

  1. Faithful reach is not free — I'd now advise against it

  You asked me to quantify before committing. I did, and the cost is severe
  enough that I'm not implementing it without you seeing this.

  Dead zone under a faithful 640×400 rect, across all 405 levels:

  ┌───────────────┬─────────────────────────┬─────────────┬───────┐
  │ logicalHeight │ levels with a dead zone │ mean dead % │ worst │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────┼─────────────┼───────┤
  │ 400           │ 0                       │ 0           │ 0     │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────┼─────────────┼───────┤
  │ 600           │ 375                     │ 33.3        │ 33.3  │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────┼─────────────┼───────┤
  │ 720           │ 375                     │ 40.6        │ 44.4  │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────┼─────────────┼───────┤
  │ 900           │ 375                     │ 43.2        │ 55.6  │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────┼─────────────┼───────┤
  │ 1385 (phone)  │ 375                     │ 43.9        │ 58.3  │
  └───────────────┴─────────────────────────┴─────────────┴───────┘

  By room height on a phone: 400-tall rooms 0% (30 levels), 600 → 33% (75), 640
  → 38% (90), 720 → 44% (120), 960 → 58% (90).

  So on a phone, on 375 of 405 levels, roughly 44% of the visible play area
  would show enemies the laser and magic refuse to touch. That's not an edge
  case, it's the median experience on the target platform.

  And the reframing that changes my answer: the AS3's 640×400 is not a balance
  number — it is the screen. The rule's purpose is "don't hit what you can't
  see." Porting the literal inverts it into "can't hit what you can see," which
  contradicts the rule's own intent. worldView is the faithful port of the rule;
  640×400 is the faithful port of the number.

  The fairness problem also isn't solvable here. A taller viewport already
  changes spawn placement, warning visibility and reaction time — weapon reach
  is one facet of a responsive camera the whole game rests on. Fixing fairness
  properly means letterboxing gameplay to a fixed aspect, which is a design
  decision, not a two-line change.

  I'll implement whichever you choose. My revised recommendation is keep 
  worldView, document it as a known viewport divergence alongside the spawn
  acceptance-rate entry. If you still want faithful, I'd pair it with the
  visualisation you mentioned — a 44% invisible dead zone needs to be visible.

  2. The blast radius is real, and worse than the laser

  checkWithinScreen: grep finds 4 sites. The rule is computed at 10. The other
  six are inlined at :1716, :1814, :1906, :4115, :4761, :5195, :5565, :6901.
  What found them was probing on cameraPosX — an operand that appears nowhere
  else in the file. Skeleton-matching whole lines found only 2 of 45 helpers and
  missed :5565 itself, because that inline drops the distanceAdd terms. So I
  can't give you a trustworthy repo-wide count; the honest answer is that the
  method to find these is per-rule operand probing, and any name-based count is
  a floor.

  The consequence you predicted is confirmed, with a concrete instance.
  enemyBehaviour's branched set is built from two idioms. A third exists —
  instance.enemy == "X", the spawn dispatch, present for all 20 types, matched
  by neither regex. And two of those spawn branches carry real behaviour: :3475
  gives Accelerating a 2.7× speed multiplier, :3479 gives Temperamental 2×.

  So a type whose only distinguishing behaviour lives in the spawn switch would
  be reported as having no branch, therefore implemented. That is exactly the 
  mechanism that put the false "no branch for this type" line into the generated
  ENEMIES.md for Exploding — same bug, and the generator still has it.

  What I have not done, and think is next: re-examine the 9 types the ledger
  calls implemented against the third idiom. If any has spawn-time behaviour,
  the ledger is optimistic again and ENEMIES.md is publishing it. That's a
  contained check and I'd rather do it deliberately than have it surface the way
  this one did.

  Teleporting's two sound cues (:4946, :4973, gated on the rect widened by 100)
  are now recorded against the SPECIAL_MECHANICS entry itself, not just the
  audit — the ledger calls that type data-only, so the audit alone wouldn't be
  seen.

  Music is queued as one unit. Say the word on the laser/magic call and whether
  you want the 9-type re-examination before or after it.

      113 +  const spawnCases = (): Map<string, string[]> => {           
      114 +    const lines = source.split(/\r?\n/);                      
      115 +    const starts: { line: number; type: string }[] = [];      
      116 +    lines.forEach((l, i) => {                                 
      117 +      const m = l.match(/instance\.enemy == "(\w+)"/);        
      118 +      // The constructor switch only; the speedMultiplier swit
          +ch is at :3475.                                               
      119 +      if (m && i + 1 >= 2934 && i + 1 <= 3246) starts.push({ l
          +ine: i, type: m[1]! });                                       
      120 +    });                                                       
      121 +    const out = new Map<string, string[]>();                  
      122 +    starts.forEach((s, k) => {                                
      123 +      const end = k + 1 < starts.length ? starts[k + 1]!.line 
          +: 3250;                                                       
      124 +      out.set(                                                
      125 +        s.type,                                               
      126 +        lines                                                 
      127 +          .slice(s.line, end)                                 
      128 +          .map((x) => x.trim())                               
      129 +          .filter(                                            
      130 +            (x) =>                                            
      131 +              x &&                                            
      132 +              x !== '{' &&                                    
      133 +              x !== '}' &&                                    
      134 +              !/^(if|else)\b/.test(x) &&                      
      135 +              !/^enemy = new /.test(x) &&                     
      136 +              !/^enemyStatsArray = /.test(x) &&               
      137 +              !/^\+\+ScreenGame\./.test(x),                   
      138 +          ),                                                  
      139 +      );                                                      
      140 +    });                                                       
      141 +    return out;                                               
      142 +  };                                                          
      143 +                                                              
      144 +  it('finds a spawn case for every type', () => {
