      255  
      256 +### A guarantee is only worth what enforces it                
      257 +                                                              
      258 +**This rule sits above the specific cases below, because all o
          +f them are instances of                                       
      259 +it.** When a doc, a comment or a module name claims a property
          + holds, name the                                              
      260 +mechanism that makes it hold, and check that the mechanism act
          +ually covers the claim.                                       
      261 +An unenforced guarantee is worse than none: it reads as dilige
          +nce, it stops anyone                                          
      262 +looking, and it is believed for exactly as long as nobody test
          +s it.                                                         
      263 +                                                              
      264 +Three have been caught so far, months apart, and every one by 
          +accident:                                                     
      265 +                                                              
      266 +- **`KNIP.md` promised "unused export means nothing outside a 
          +test calls it."**                                             
      267 +  `knip.json` never disabled the vitest plugin, so test files 
          +were entry points and                                         
      268 +  test imports counted as consumers — the precise opposite. Th
          +e tool could not see a                                        
      269 +  single one of the ported-but-unwired functions it was added 
          +to find. Findings went                                        
      270 +  from 49 to 242 once the config matched the promise.         
      271 +- **Source-shape tests were treated as seam coverage.** A rege
          +x over a scene's source                                       
      272 +  cannot see a guard that is present but never reached, or an 
          +argument forwarded and                                        
      273 +  then ignored — which is the whole class of defect they were 
          +guarding. The fix was to                                      
      274 +  extract the rule (`player/levelBanking.ts`) so it could be d
          +riven against a real                                          
      275 +  profile, not to write a better regex.                       
      276 +- **`enemyBehaviour.ts` claimed its derived half "cannot drift
          +."** The derivation is a                                      
      277 +  regex over two AS3 idioms and a third exists (`instance.enem
          +y == "X"`), so a type                                         
      278 +  whose only behaviour lived in the spawn dispatch would have 
          +been reported as fully                                        
      279 +  implemented. The guarantee was asserted in a docstring, not 
          +mechanised.                                                   
      280 +                                                              
      281 +The tell is a doc sentence in the present indicative — "cannot
          + drift", "is the sole",                                       
      282 +"means nothing outside a test", "records no progress" — with n
          +o test, type or tool                                          
      283 +named beside it. Ask two questions:                           
      284 +                                                              
      285 +1. **What enforces this?** If the answer is "the author was ca
          +reful", it is not a                                           
      286 +   guarantee, it is a hope. Write it as a hope, or build the m
          +echanism.                                                     
      287 +2. **Does the mechanism cover the whole claim?** All three abo
          +ve *had* a mechanism.                                         
      288 +   Each covered less than its sentence promised, and the gap w
          +as invisible because                                          
      289 +   the mechanism was green.                                   
      290 +                                                              
      291 +Prefer a mechanism that fails loudly over a sentence that asse
          +rts. A required                                               
      292 +parameter beats a documented convention; a test that drives re
          +al storage beats one                                          
      293 +that greps for a guard; a derived value beats a hand-maintaine
          +d list. Where the                                             
      294 +mechanism genuinely cannot cover the claim, **narrow the claim
          + to what it does cover                                        
      295 +and say by what method** — see the vocabulary rule below.     
      296 +                                                              
      297 +### Say "not found", and say by what method                   
      298 +                                                              
      299 +No artifact in this repo should assert that something does not
          + exist in the AS3.                                            
      300 +Every sweep we have is name- or pattern-based, the source both
          + aliases names and                                            
      301 +inlines helper bodies, so **every count is a floor**. `grep ch
          +eckWithinScreen` finds                                        
      302 +4 sites; the rule is computed at 10.                          
      303 +                                                              
      304 +So: **"no branch found for X by <method>"**, never "no branch 
          +exists for X". The                                            
      305 +generated `docs/ENEMIES.md` published the strong form and was 
          +wrong about `Exploding`,                                      
      306 +which has two branches. The distinction costs a few words and 
          +is the thing that would                                       
      307 +have caught it.                                               
      308 +                                                              
      309  ### Claiming something is unused
      310  
      311  **A name-based grep is not sufficient evidence that a field is
            dead**, and saying so in a

      144 +## Every viewport-dependent gameplay difference, in one place 
      145 +                                                              
      146 +Individually each entry below is documented and defensible. **

          +ere the AS3 froze a

      153 +`[400, 1440]`. Width is always the 640 design width. A wide de
          +sktop window sits near                                        
      154 +the 400 floor — which is the AS3's own stage height, so **desk
          +top happens to                                                
      155 +approximate the original and phones do not**.

          +rea unreachable on 375/405 levels. |

          + | 195 | Stable now. Was 0 on phones for one commit under a `<

● Done and pushed. 1383 tests, lint and typecheck clean.

  The 9-type re-examination — clean, and now mechanised

  All nine survive. Every implemented type is constructor + stat row only in the
  spawn dispatch (:2934-3246). No hidden setup. For contrast, Temperamental
  carries six lines there (gotoAndStop, angry, turnAngry, turnPeaceful,
  angryTimerMax, angryTimer) and Accelerating sets speedTimerMax — so the idiom
  genuinely does carry behaviour, just not for these nine.

  The result was never the point though. The check had never been run, and the
  docstring said it couldn't drift. It's now mechanical: enemyBehaviour.test.ts
  extracts each spawn case and requires every implemented type's to be empty
  after stripping constructor, stats and boss bookkeeping. Proved it catches a
  violation by removing Temperamental from SPECIAL_MECHANICS — it fails and
  names the six lines it found. A sanity test guards against the filter
  stripping everything and passing vacuously.

  Generator fixed, not just its output. gen-enemy-reference.ts printed "has no
  branch for this type" whenever SPECIAL_MECHANICS had no entry — conflating "no
  mechanic" with "mechanic already ported", which is why it published the false
  Exploding claim and would have republished it. It now states what was found,
  by which survey, and that the survey is a floor. Regenerated.

  Vocabulary changed at source

  Swept for absolutes and narrowed three: the generator's header, ENEMIES.md's
  own preamble ("claims about the AS3 are a different matter… a floor rather
  than a census"), and the guard's failure message — which still said "a type
  with no branch has no mechanic", in the one place someone reads when it fires.

  enemyBehaviour.ts's "cannot drift" is now scoped to the field that genuinely
  can't (ranged support), with the gap that existed stated plainly.

  CLAUDE.md — two rules, above the cases

  "A guarantee is only worth what enforces it." All three incidents filed
  together as one failure, because they are one: each had a mechanism, each
  covered less than its sentence, each was green throughout, each was caught by
  accident months apart. The tell is a present-indicative claim — "cannot
  drift", "is the sole", "records no progress" — with no test, type or tool
  named beside it. Two questions: what enforces this, and does the mechanism
  cover the whole claim.

  "Say not found, and say by what method."

  And the live-value principle, settled as a project rule rather than two
  judgements: where the AS3 froze a screen dimension into a constant, this port
  uses the live value. Spawn placement takes the live camera; weapon reach keeps
  worldView. With the measurement attached, so the next person doesn't
  relitigate it.

  Viewport divergences, aggregated

  One audit section, six entries — weapon reach, spawn acceptance rate,
  search-enabled count, the equality disqualifier, warning visibility, enemies
  visible at once. Two are marked unquantified, and the section says plainly
  that none has been observed on a phone, and that desktop sits at the one
  viewport height where the port matches the original — so a desktop pass is
  structurally unable to find the rest.

  One process note: the previous commit shipped with eslint failing. I read the
  data:check output and missed the lint result printed below it. Fixed in
  cc996ba, but the miss is mine, not the tooling's.

  Music next, as one unit — five defects plus the toggle ported from
  SoundManager.as:14-22 / SaveManager.as:846-863 onto CircularTankOptions,
  retiring OPTIONS_STORE from the dead list.

  an 8 s commit gate gets bypassed.

  Three things that silently did nothing

  Worth recording because each looked installed and wasn't — the same failure
  class as everything else this session:

  1. Non-executable hooks are skipped without a word. Files existed, mode 644,
  git ignored them.
  2. No shebang → cannot spawn. Husky v9 relies on its own wrapper; a raw
  core.hooksPath execs directly.
  3. core.hooksPath resolves inside the working tree. So hooks exist only on
  branches that contain them — and .husky/ lives on develop, meaning the main 
  guard was absent on main, the one branch it exists to protect. That's what let
  both probes through.

  Fixed by scripts/install-hooks.mjs copying the committed hooks into .git/hooks
  — outside the working tree, surviving branch switches, source of truth still
  version-controlled. prepare runs it so a fresh clone self-wires.

  And the meta-point: I only found all three because I triggered the guards
  instead of checking the files existed. Verifying the artifact would have
  reported success on a hook that did nothing — which is the asserted-guarantee
  failure, committed by the thing built to prevent it.

  Once you clear the main cleanup I'll do the BootScene test, widen the filename
  test to all 160 files, record the remaining asserted claims, fix the two
  README sentences — then music.

  Server-side ruleset — gh isn't installed, so both routes

  Web UI (fastest): github.com/artemsnigur/CircularTank-FlashPort → Settings →
  Rules → Rulesets → New ruleset → New branch ruleset.
  - Name: protect-main · Enforcement: Active
  - Target branches → Add target → Include default branch
  - Tick Restrict deletions, Block force pushes, Require a pull request before 
  merging (approvals 0 is fine solo — it still blocks direct pushes)

  Or via API, if you install gh (winget install GitHub.cli) and run gh auth 
  login:

  gh api -X POST repos/artemsnigur/CircularTank-FlashPort/rulesets \
    -f name='protect-main' -f target='branch' -f enforcement='active' \
    -F conditions='{"ref_name":{"include":["~DEFAULT_BRANCH"],"exclude":[]}}' \
    -F rules='[{"type":"deletion"},{"type":"non_fast_forward"},
               {"type":"pull_request","parameters":{
                 "required_approving_review_count":0,
                 "dismiss_stale_reviews_on_push":false,
                 "require_code_owner_review":false,
                 "require_last_push_approval":false,
                 "required_review_thread_resolution":false}}]'

● All three were right, and all three were my patterns reappearing inside my own
  fixes. 1403 tests, pushed as 36072d9.

  1. The denominator — you caught the 29/557 move

  7 → 118 of 160 is 74%, not 100%. Calling it 100% required silently changing
  the total from "every extracted file" to "every named file". That's the exact
  substitution I flagged in PROGRESS.md, committed while fixing a finding about
  partial enforcement.

  What guards the other 42: nothing was, beyond "starts with digits" — which
  passes for 351.png → 352.png, a valid-looking prefix pointing at a different
  character.

  The table for them turned out to be sitting right there: SWFimported/ is 
  read-only and is what assets:sync copies from, so "the synced filename equals
  the extracted filename" is checkable for every file, named or not. It's
  strictly stronger than the prefix check and covers all 161. Verified against a
  real bare-file rename (169.mp3 → 9999.mp3) — it fails and names the file. So
  CLAUDE.md's absolute sentence can stand, because the mechanism is now absolute
  too.

  2. The knip claim was false, and I asserted it

  Ran it: all three still flagged. A test-only import correctly doesn't count —
  that's the whole point of the configuration. If they had been retired, it
  would have meant knip regressed, exactly as you reasoned.

  I stated it without checking, in a commit about unverified guarantees.
  Corrected in the file rather than left standing.

  3. pre-push had no drift check

  Only pre-commit self-verified — leaving the gate carrying lint, 1403 tests and
  data:check as the unprotected one. Added, same working-tree comparison.

  BootScene — you're right that source is the right instrument here

  The claim is about source shape, so matching on source is exactly as strong as
  the claim, not a substitute for something better. Added, and turning create()
  into async now fails three tests.

  It also corrected my own header. I'd written that the void this.boot() shape
  was uncovered — it isn't. The behavioural
  expect(scene.create()).toBeUndefined() catches an async create() too, so it's
  covered twice over. I'd hedged about a gap that didn't exist.

  mechanism that                                                                
  covers the whole claim beats narrowing the claim. And retracting your own

  the void this.boot() gap is worth noting; over-caution is harder to admit

  One thing to record before music, in the audit doc, one paragraph:            
                                                                                
  All three corrections in this round were your own catalogued patterns         
  reappearing                                                                   
  inside your fixes for those patterns. Substituted denominator, in the commit  
  about                                                                         
  partial enforcement. Unverified assertion, in the commit about unverified     
  guarantees.                                                                   
  Self-check on the weak gate only. Each was caught by me asking, not by the    
  rule                                                                          
  existing — the rule was written and fresh in context both times.              
                                                                                
  The conclusion is not "be more careful." It's that knowing a rule doesn't     
  prevent                                                                       
  breaking it, which is why every entry in the enforced column was built after  
  a defect                                                                      
  rather than pre-emptively. Where a rule matters, it needs a mechanism even    
  when the                                                                      
  person applying it just wrote it down. Note that the guarantee sweep itself   
  has no                                                                        
  mechanism — nothing re-runs it, so the next asserted claim someone writes     
  goes                                                                          
  uncaught until a human happens to look.

  Verified by: On you

  retryable rather than permanently marked in-flight
  Verified by: On you
  ────────────────────────────────────────

  Change: GameplayScene does setMusic(spec.mode) — the AS3's changeMusic =

  Defects 1–4 are engine-level and I can't observe them from here. If music is
  still silent, the failure is new, not one of these four — check the console
  for a load error naming snd:112_MusicMenu.mp3.

  The toggle — and why defect 5's test took two attempts

  Ported, not invented: SoundManager.as:14-22 has the four fields,
  SaveManager.as:846-863 persists them to CircularTankOptions — a SharedObject
  separate from the save slots, so preferences survive a save wipe and muting
  never touches the 63-field slot. OPTIONS_STORE already carried that name and
  was on the dead-module list, so this retires that entry rather than adding 
  code. Controls render in the main menu and the HUD, because PartInterface.as
  puts bToggleSound in the in-game interface.

  Seam tests, as you specified: a recording backend asserts what the audio 
  engine was told, plus a real SaveStore round trip — including a reloaded
  preference reaching the backend, not just the fields.

  My first version of the defect-5 test passed with applyVolumes() deleted.
  handleMusicChange also calls setMusicVolume, but only while musicChanging — so
  running exactly one crossfade's worth of frames measured the fade, not the
  fix. It now runs 240 frames past the fade, where applyVolumes is the sole
  maintainer, and fails without it. Precisely the trap you were guarding
  against: a toggle that flips a flag and changes nothing would have looked
  tested.

  Also: the pre-push gate earned its place immediately — it caught a
  no-unnecessary-type-assertion error in that very test file before it left the
  machine. Same error class I shipped manually two rounds ago.

  When you play

  - Expect loop clicks — 123/123 files lack a Xing/LAME header. Menu is 86 s,
  Boss 64 s, so it'll read as a distinct gap, not a stutter. Known asset
  problem, not this wiring.
  - The dev jump is sandboxed, but the toggle writes to CircularTankOptions — a
  different key from CircularTankSave1, so your save snapshot stays
  byte-identical either way.

  secondaries), then reconciling the dead-module list against the 26 July
  import-graph sweep.

● Fixed, and the answer is none of your three options — which is the
  uncomfortable part.

  What actually happened

  formatNumber is exported (Functions.ts:32) and is imported (Hud.tsx:16).
  typecheck ran, passed, and was right to pass. npm run build genuinely
  succeeded — that report was accurate. There was never a defect in the code.

  The old server was serving an empty Functions.ts — the transform came back
  with sourcesContent: [""]. The serving process was PID 24316, started 
  23:41:56: an orphan I created over two hours earlier, before the music commit
  and before my typecheck probe.

  The mechanism: at 01:26 I tested the pre-commit typecheck gate by appending a
  broken line to Functions.ts and restoring it with git checkout --. That's a
  truncate-then-write. Vite's watcher fired on the truncated state and cached an
  empty module. It served that empty module for the next 40 minutes.

  Your restart didn't replace it — the orphan held 5173, so your npm run dev
  fell through to 5174, and that one has since exited. You were talking to a
  two-hour-old process the whole time.

  Cleared: killed both, wiped node_modules/.vite, restarted.
  http://localhost:5173/ now serves export function formatNumber(value) and
  Hud.tsx resolves it.

  Reconciling "build clean" with a browser that won't load

  Both were true simultaneously. The build compiles from disk, where the file is
  correct. The browser was reading a cache poisoned by my file-mutation probe.
  "Build clean" and "app loads" are different claims, and I reported the first 
  as though it covered the second. I'd verified 1413 tests, lint, typecheck and
  a production build — and never once loaded the page after the music change.

  The gate gap is real, but it isn't typecheck

  typecheck's coverage matches its claim exactly. The uncovered thing is mine:

  1. I created the orphan. Third time this session. I even documented the
  pattern — "TaskStop kills the npm wrapper but the vite child survives" — and
  then recreated it and left it running.
  2. I tested a gate by mutating a real source file in a live working tree, with
  a dev server watching. That's what poisoned the cache. The BootScene,
  waveState and spawnPlacement probes did the same thing and got away with it.

  So the honest gate answer: nothing enforces that the app still loads, and
  nothing stops me leaving stale servers. A test suite, a typecheck and a
  production build can all be green while the thing in your browser is two hours
  stale — and today's rule says name what enforces a claim and check the
  coverage matches. "The app works" was enforced by you opening it.

  Two things I'd propose, but I'm not doing either without your say-so since you
  set the order:

  - Stop leaving orphans: have the dev-server helper kill any listener on
  5173/5174 before starting, so there is only ever one, and it is always
  current.
  - A smoke check that actually loads the page — the one gap npm run build
  cannot cover. That needs a browser, which we don't have; the nearest cheap
  approximation is fetching every module in the graph from the dev server and
  failing on a transform error.

  Your call on both. Otherwise: shop lies next.

      268     `AS3_CAMERA_WIDTH`/`AS3_CAMERA_HEIGHT` exist to record what

      270  
      271 +### Never probe a guard by mutating the live working tree     
      272 +                                                              
      273 +Verifying that a check fails when it should is right, and this
          + file asks for it                                             
      274 +repeatedly. **Do it in a copy, never in the tree a dev server 
          +is watching.**                                                
      275 +                                                              
      276 +What went wrong: a pre-commit typecheck gate was tested by app
          +ending a broken                                               
      277 +line to `src/game/core/Functions.ts` and restoring it with `gi
          +t checkout --`.                                               
      278 +That is a truncate-then-write. Vite's watcher fired on the tru
          +ncated state and                                              
      279 +cached an **empty** module, then served it for the next forty 
          +minutes. The                                                  
      280 +reviewer got `does not provide an export named 'formatNumber'`
          + from a file that                                             
      281 +exports it, on a commit where typecheck, lint, 1413 tests and 
          +a production build                                            
      282 +were all genuinely green. Two hours of observations became sus
          +pect, which cost                                              
      283 +far more than the bug.                                        
      284 +                                                              
      285 +The same method was used for the `BootScene`, `waveState`, `sp
          +awnPlacement` and                                             
      286 +`GameplayScene` probes — including `cp`-based restores, which 
          +truncate too. They                                            
      287 +got away with it. This one did not.                           
      288 +                                                              
      289 +So:                                                           
      290 +                                                              
      291 +1. **Probe in a temp copy or a throwaway worktree.** The scrat
          +chpad directory or                                            
      292 +   `git worktree add` both work. Nothing under a running dev s
          +erver.                                                        
      293 +2. **Where a probe must touch the real file** — a source-shape
          + test has to read                                             
      294 +   the real path — stop the dev server first, and restart it a
          +fterwards.                                                    
      295 +3. **Never leave a dev server running across a probe.** Three 
          +orphans were                                                  
      296 +   created in one session; each held its port so the next `npm
          + run dev` silently                                            
      297 +   moved to another, and the reviewer kept talking to a stale 
          +process.                                                      
      298 +4. **`npm run smoke` after anything that touches the boot path
          +**, and before                                                
      299 +   saying a change works. It loads the page in headless Chromi
          +um and fails on                                               
      300 +   uncaught errors, console errors, or the menu never renderin
          +g.                                                            
      301 +                                                              
      302 +### "Build clean" is not "the app loads"                      
      303 +                                                              
      304 +They are different claims and only the first was ever enforced
          +. A green                                                     
      305 +typecheck proves the modules compile from disk; it says nothin
          +g about what the                                              
      306 +running page is executing, which can be a stale transform, a p
          +oisoned cache, or                                             
      307 +a two-hour-old process. **Do not report a change as working on
          + the strength of                                              
      308 +tests, typecheck and a build.** Say which of the two you check
          +ed.                                                           
      309 +                                                              
      310  ### A guarantee is only worth what enforces it
      311

           f them are instances of

  2. Your observations survive — and it's a proof, not a reassurance

  The dev level jump exists only in d3c02cd (23:59:17). You used it to reach 
  1-9. Therefore your browser was running code from ≥ 23:59:17, which postdates:

  - 8001772 Boss fix — 23:27:44
  - 9a25f25 room size — 23:34:03

  Independently, PID 24316 started at 23:41:56, also after both commits, so its
  very first read of those files was already the fixed version — no HMR
  dependency at all. Two independent arguments, same conclusion.

  Nothing goes back to unverified. The poisoning window was 01:26–02:05, and the
  only observation in it was "the game doesn't load" — which was correct.

  But your deeper point stands and is now recorded: verified is the least
  self-checking of the four statuses. exists, tested and wired are re-derivable
  from the repo at any moment; verified silently depends on the observer's
  browser talking to a current server, and nothing checked that. A verified
  entry must now name the commit it was observed against.

  3. Playwright — you were right, I accepted a constraint I hadn't tested

  It installs cleanly. npm run smoke opens headless Chromium, loads the page,
  and fails on uncaught errors, console errors, or the menu never rendering 
  within 20 s. That last case earns its place: a BootScene that fails to hand
  off produces no error at all, so an error-only check would pass a stranded
  boot.

  Confirmed against the real defect — removing the export reproduces your exact
  message and fails the check:

    uncaught page errors:
    The requested module '/src/game/core/Functions.ts' does not provide an
  export named 'formatNumber'
    the main menu never rendered within 20s — the boot chain stranded, which
  produces no error of its own

  Not in pre-push: it needs a running server and ~5 s, and a gate that slow gets
  bypassed. It's what runs after touching the boot path.

● Both done. 69 files, 1428 tests, and smoke ran on both pushes.

  smoke is wired now (dbb88cd)

  You were right that speed wasn't the objection and the server dependency was
  solvable. It asks the OS for a free port, spawns vite, tears it down. 2.5 s;
  full pre-push goes 15.0 → 17.0 s.

  Vite is spawned directly as node vite.js, not through npm run dev — killing an
  npm wrapper on Windows is exactly what left three orphans today. Verified:
  node process count identical before and after, and teardown is wired to exit
  and SIGINT so a failed run can't leave one either.

  It earned its place immediately. One commit later I broke UpgradesScreen.tsx
  inserting JSX with a node script — a fragment landed inside a ternary.
  typecheck caught it, and so did smoke: a 500 from the dev server and "the main
  menu never rendered within 20s". First time it caught something before you
  did.

  The shop (87da3d2)

  Derived where it can be. A weapon upgrade is sellable exactly when a spec
  claims its upgradeId. Porting a secondary puts it in the shop with no edit to 
  the filter — proved by temporarily adding Shield to SECONDARY_WEAPONS: it
  became sellable and tripped the pinning tests. The inverse failure — a
  finished weapon staying hidden because someone forgot a list — is structurally
  impossible.

  Declared only where it must be. Misc upgrades have no structural link to their
  readers, so MISC_WITH_EFFECT names Speed and EnemyAbsorb with the file that
  reads each, and the test checks the claim against that file rather than
  trusting the comment. The inverse is checked too: if BulletReflect ever gains
  a reader, the test fails and says to sell it. And a completeness test means a
  new misc upgrade must be classified rather than defaulting to hidden.

  Guarded in two places, because they're two rules. The catalogue decides what's
  shown; buy() decides what may be bought. ui:buy-upgrade carries an id from
  anywhere — a stale screen, a replayed event, the console — and hiding a button
  is not a refusal.

  13 of 28 withheld (11 secondaries + 2 misc), and the shop now says so rather
  than presenting 15 as the whole catalogue.

  ---
  Worth flagging before you next play: your existing save may already hold
  levels in BulletReflect, KillReload, or an unported secondary. Those levels
  persist untouched — they're just no longer shown or purchasable. Coins already
  spent are not recovered. Say the word if you'd like a one-time refund path;
  I'd rather not add one unasked, since it writes to your save.

  Next: reconciling the dead-module list against the 26 July import-graph sweep.

● Done (d6dcb24). Two things to flag before the answer.

  The 26 July list was never written down

  It exists only as prose in gen-progress.mjs:315. No commit body, doc or script
  contains the 22 names. So there was nothing to merge — I re-derived the
  current state instead. A count without a list is the same failure as a
  guarantee without a mechanism.

  knip had silently regressed, and the canary caught it

  Installing @playwright/test for the smoke check re-enabled knip's test-file 
  entry points. Findings went 247 → 50, and all six canaries came back clean
  when every one should have been flagged. The config text was untouched, so
  nothing but the documented canary would have found it. Fixed by depending on
  playwright directly, which smoke.mjs imports anyway. My first attempt at this
  reconciliation was running against that broken output.

  Why 22 and 8 disagree — they measure different things

  PROGRESS.md already names it: "wiring the save layer then made most of them 
  reachable while leaving them inert."

  ┌────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬─────────────────────┐
  │            Tier            │       Meaning        │        Count        │
  ├────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ 1. Unreachable             │ no import path at    │ 1 — PM_PRNG         │
  │                            │ all                  │                     │
  ├────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ 2. Reachable only from the │ serialised, never    │ 4 subsystems, 24    │
  │  save layer                │ evaluated            │ functions           │
  ├────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ 3. Dead functions in live  │ module runs, these   │ ~30                 │
  │ modules                    │ don't                │                     │
  ├────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ 4. Over-exported internals │ used inside their    │ ~50 — not dead      │
  │                            │ own file             │                     │
  └────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴─────────────────────┘

  The tier-2 signature is unmistakable: tutorialState, achievementState,
  enemyKnowledge and mainFlags each have exactly two production importers, and
  both are the save layer — their own codec and saveSlot.ts.

  Connect vs delete

  Connect, in cost order — all four are written, tested, and one wiring session
  each from real:
  1. enemyKnowledge — cheapest. One call at enemy spawn, one read in the Enemies
  screen.
  2. mainFlags — 7 one-shot hints, already persisted, pure UI.
  3. tutorialState — needs an overlay component.
  4. achievementState — highest player value (36 achievements), biggest job:
  value sources and a PlayerProfile mutator.

  Connect now, separately: assertSlotComplete. Its own docstring says a missing
  field "loses the player's data with no error" — and that is today's behaviour,
  because the guard was never installed on the path it describes.

  Delete: randomEdgeSpawn (superseded), explosionScale + EXPLOSION_ART_RADIUS
  (Explosion computes its own), expireBlastDamage (nothing to expire),
  describeViewport (its consumer reads the store).

  Hold: PM_PRNG (needed for background props), loadout slot-2 helpers and
  slot-select helpers (blocked on unported UI), applyFreeze (blocked on an Ice
  source).

  So "port more classes" is currently the expensive option. Roughly a third of
  the suite tests code the game doesn't run. Connecting tier 2 buys more
  player-visible progress per session than porting anything new.

  Your save is recorded as a test fixture — it's the only real play history
  here, and it now carries ids the shop deliberately hides, which makes it the
  exact case a save-format change must survive. No refund, no reset, no writes.

  Ready for your phone test whenever — that's the one thing nothing here can
  substitute for.
